# By Imperial Decree: Complete Library of the Four Treasuries

The mention of the "time of intersection" <note>referring to the *hui-shuo* or dark-to-new moon transition mentioned on the previous page</note> serves as a metaphor. It does not mean that one can only perform the practice during the brief interval between the dark moon and the new moon each month.

The *Chapters on Awakening to the Truth* <note>original: *Wuzhen pian* (悟眞篇), a seminal 11th-century text on internal alchemy</note> states: "The sun and moon meet once every thirty days; use the hours to replace the days to model the divine work." If a student can understand the subtle mystery of "using hours to replace days" <note>This refers to the alchemical "Small Heavenly Circulation," where the practitioner compresses a month's worth of cosmic energy movement into the hours of a single day’s meditation.</note>, they can seize the hidden mechanisms of Heaven and Earth to complete the Great Elixir <term>Da dan (大丹): the refined state of immortal essence</term>.

## Virile Yang sows the dark seed; Feminine Yin governs the golden transformation.

"Virile Yang sows the dark seed" <note>original: *Xiongyang bo xuan shi* (雄陽播玄施). *Xuan* (dark/mysterious) is the color associated with Heaven.</note> refers to the breath of Heaven descending until it reaches the Earth. "Feminine Yin governs the golden transformation" <note>original: *Ciyin tong huang hua* (雌陰統黃化). *Huang* (yellow/golden) is the color associated with the Earth.</note> refers to the Earth receiving the breath of Heaven to generate life. 

The *Chapters on Awakening to the Truth* says: "Sweet dew falls when Heaven and Earth unite; yellow sprouts <note>A metaphor for the first signs of the alchemical elixir forming.</note> grow when Pit and Fire intersect." <note>original: *Kan-Li jiao* (坎離交). *Kan* (the Pit/Water) and *Li* (Fire) are hexagrams representing the internal energies of the kidneys and heart.</note> Generally, the method of "generating the medicine" in alchemy is identical to the way Heaven and Earth generate all living things. It is nothing more than the two breaths <term>Qi (氣): life force or energetic breath</term> of Yin and Yang—one sowing and one transforming—as the Dark of Heaven and the Golden of Earth intersect.

# Primal Chaos Intersects to Establish the Foundation

The two breaths <note>or "vital energies" (Qi)</note> of Yin and Yang intersect above and below, mixing to become one; therefore, it is called "Primal Chaos" <note>original: *Hundun* (渾沌), the state of undifferentiated unity before the creation of the universe.</note>. Primal Chaos is the border and ridge <term>Yine (鄞鄂): a technical term in alchemy referring to the boundary or the "earthenware mold" in which the elixir is shaped; it represents the structural foundation of the internal work</term> of Heaven and Earth, and the womb of the myriad things. The methods of the Elixir <note>original: *Danfa* (丹法)</note> use this as the beginning <note>original: *Quanyu* (權輿)</note> to establish the foundation. Thus, Heaven, Earth, and all things are contained within my own body, and there is no need to seek them from the outside.

### Managing and Nourishing the Border and Ridge; Concentrating Spirit to Form the Body

The "border and ridge" is the very root and stem of life. The alchemical method operates within this space; by turning the light around to shine inward <note>original: *huiguang neizhao* (回光内照), a meditative practice of redirecting the focus of consciousness from external distractions back toward the inner self</note>, the Spirit clings to the Breath and congeals, while the Breath clings to the Spirit and remains still. Naturally, they bind together to form the "Embryo." If the light of the Spirit leaks outward and the "horse of the breath" <note>a metaphor for the restless, wandering nature of one's vital energy</note> gallops away, then the Sun and Moon will lose their path, and the Metal and Mercury <note>original: *Jin-gong* (金汞), internal symbols for the refined essences of the lungs/metal and heart/liquid</note> will be in different furnaces. To hope for the completion of the Elixir under such circumstances is far-fetched indeed.

<summary>This page defines the state of Primal Chaos as the union of Yin and Yang within the body, serving as the essential foundation for internal alchemy. It instructs the practitioner to "turn the light inward" to bind spirit and breath, preventing vital energy from dissipating.</summary>
<keywords>Primal Chaos (Hundun), Border and Ridge (Yine), Internal Alchemy (Neidan), Turning the Light Inward (Huiguang Neizhao), Spirit and Breath, Metal and Mercury, Alchemical Embryo</keywords>

...as noted in the *Song of the Pearl, Jade Leaves, and the Marrow of the Elixir* <note>original: *Zhu yu ye dan sui ge* (珠玉葉丹髓歌)</note> by Bai Ziqing <note>also known as Bai Yuchan, a famous 13th-century master of the Southern Lineage of Internal Alchemy</note>: "In the past, I met my master and received the oral transmission in person; it only requires concentrating the Spirit and entering the Breath Hole <term>Qixue (氣穴): The 'Aperture of the Breath,' a vital center in the lower abdomen where the practitioner focuses their internal energy to catalyze transformation.</term>." This is why it is called the "Divine Elixir" <term>Shendan (神丹): The 'Spiritual Elixir,' representing the refined state of one's own life force and consciousness.</term> and why one is titled a "Divine Immortal" <term>Shenxian (神仙): A realized being who has attained spiritual perfection and transcendence through Daoist practice.</term>.

->**All things coil to emerge; from wriggling insects, none do not follow [this Way].**<-

The Way of the intersection of Yin and Yang is the very source of endless birth and transformation. It is not only humans, birds, and beasts that emerge from this; even the tiniest creatures—those that fly, wriggle, walk on feet, or breathe through beaks—all of them without exception are thus. Therefore, it is said: "All things coil to emerge; from wriggling insects, none do not follow this Way." 

If this principle is applied to others <note>meaning, directed outward toward another person</note>, it generates human beings; if it is preserved within oneself, it generates the Medicine <note>the 'internal elixir' or 'medicine' (yao) refers to the refined vital energies used for self-cultivation</note>. The only difference lies in the distinction between the Sage and the common person, and between the "Reverse" and the "Forward" <note>internal alchemy is often called the 'Reverse' (ni) path because it turns the creative energy of procreation backward and inward to restore youth and spirit, whereas the 'Forward' (shun) path leads to the birth of children and eventual death.</note> paths.

Thus, Confucius <note>original: *Zhongni* (仲尼)</note> praised the Primordial Chaos <note>original: *Hongmeng* (鴻濛), the undifferentiated state before the universe began</note>; the virtues of Heaven and Earth <note>original: *Qian* and *Kun* (乾坤), the primary hexagrams representing pure Yang and pure Yin</note> penetrate the Void; the records of antiquity praise the Primordial Emperor; and the poem "The Ospreys" <note>original: *Guanju* (關雎), the opening poem of the *Classic of Poetry*, which celebrates the harmonious union of male and female</note> establishes the beginning.

# Four Hundred Words on the Golden Elixir: Volume One

### ->At the first capping and marriage, the *Qi* becomes entwined; in the first year, the sprout flourishes.<-

I have observed that the *Book of Changes* <note>original: *Yi* (易)</note> begins with the hexagrams *Qian* and *Kun* <term>Qian and Kun (乾坤): The two primary hexagrams representing pure Yang/Heaven and pure Yin/Earth, the father and mother of all other hexagrams.</term>; the *Book of Documents* <note>original: *Shu* (書)</note> speaks of "examining antiquity" <note>original: *jigu* (稽古)</note> as its opening; the *Classic of Poetry* <note>original: *Shi* (詩)</note> places "The Osprey's Cry" <note>The *Guanju* (關雎) is the first poem of the collection, celebrating the virtuous union of a prince and his bride.</note> at the forefront; the *Book of Rites* <note>original: *Li* (禮)</note> considers the capping <note>a coming-of-age ceremony for young men</note> and marriage ceremonies as the most weighty matters; and the *Spring and Autumn Annals* <note>original: *Chunqiu* (春秋)</note> takes the "First Year" <note>the start of a ruler's reign</note> as its primary principle. 

From this, we know that when the Sages composed the classics, they all established a specific starting point. If one practices the refinement of the Elixir <term>Liandan (煉丹): The internal alchemical process of purifying one's vital essence and spirit.</term> without investigating its beginning, how can one clearly understand the play of Yin and Yang or deeply grasp the workings of Creation <term>Zaohua (造化): The transformative power of nature and the universe.</term>?

The Sage does not live in vain; looking upward, he observes the manifestation of the Heavenly Talisman <term>Tianfu (天符): The 'Heavenly Tally' or 'Talisman,' referring to the cyclical movements of the sun and moon which the alchemist must synchronize with.</term>. The Heavenly Talisman has advances and retreats, contractions and expansions, in order to correspond with the seasons. 

"Talisman" <note>original: *fu* (符)</note> means "to join" or "to harmonize." As the moon travels across the heavens, once every month it aligns and joins in union with the sun; therefore, it is called the Heavenly Talisman. That the Heavenly Talisman has "advances and retreats, contractions and expansions to correspond with the seasons" refers to the moon's light gradually [increasing] from the first day of the month onward...

<summary>This page argues that all spiritual and classical endeavors must have a defined beginning, comparing the start of alchemical practice to the opening themes of the Confucian Classics. It introduces the concept of the "Heavenly Talisman" (the sun-moon cycle) as the essential timer for internal transformation.</summary>

### Elucidation of the Seal of the Unity of the Three

Advancing the heavenly soul and strengthening the earthly soul, the waning of the light and the waxing of the dark <note>literally "the fading of Yang and the extension of Yin"</note>—this is symbolized by the period in a single day from the hour of the Rat to the hour of the Snake <term>Zi to Si (子至巳): The period from 11:00 PM to 11:00 AM, representing the rising of Yang energy.</term>. From the sixteenth day of the lunar month onward, the light gradually recedes; the earthly soul grows while the heavenly soul fades, and the dark extends while the light contracts. This is symbolized by the period in a single day from the hour of the Horse to the hour of the Pig <term>Wu to Hai (午至亥): The period from 11:00 AM to 11:00 PM, representing the descent of Yang and the rise of Yin.</term>.

The Perfected Ones <note>original: *Zhiren* (至人), those who have achieved the highest state of Taoist realization</note> of antiquity observed the Way of Heaven and grasped the movements of the celestial bodies. Consequently, they borrowed the cycles of the "Heavenly Talisman" <term>Heavenly Talisman (天符): The natural timing and celestial synchronization of the sun, moon, and stars.</term>—the advancing and retreating, the expanding and contracting of Yin and Yang—to establish the imagery of the **Firing Process** <term>Huohou (火候): Literally "fire timing," the crucial alchemical art of knowing when to increase or decrease the "heat" of one's internal cultivation.</term> as a guide for others.

For Heaven and Earth are solemnly arranged like a single **Tripod Vessel** <term>Dingqi (鼎器): An alchemical cauldron; here, it refers to the universe itself as the container for transformation.</term>, while the Sun and the Moon are the **Medicinal Ingredients**. The movement of the Sun and Moon between Heaven and Earth, their coming and going, their appearing and disappearing—this is the Firing Process itself. If a person can look back within their own body to seek these same principles, they will naturally be able to silently comprehend the wonders of the advancing and retreating of the "fire."

Therefore, the *Book of Changes* <note>original: *Yi* (易)</note> governs the "Heart of Heaven" <note>The Heart of Heaven refers to the point of absolute stillness and the birth of the first Yang energy, associated with the Winter Solstice.</note>, and the hexagram **Return** <note>original: *Fu* (復), the 24th hexagram</note> is established at the very beginning.

The "Changes" refers to the interaction of one Yin and one Yang. The Heart of Heaven is located in the North, at the center of the hour of the Rat <note>original: *Zi* (子)</note>. The hexagram *Return* corresponds to the eleventh month...

# The Complete Works of the Imperial Sanction, Volume I

### Elucidation of the Seal of the Unity of the Three

This is the hexagram of the month. The **Hexagram of Return** <term>Fu (復): The 24th hexagram of the I Ching, consisting of one Yang line at the bottom and five Yin lines above, symbolizing the return of light and life.</term> corresponds exactly to the North, at the center of the position of the **Rat** <term>Zi (子): The first of the twelve earthly branches, associated with the North, midnight, and the initial movement of Yang energy.</term>; its vital energy corresponds to the **Winter Solstice**.

Shao Kangjie <note>original: Shao Yong (邵雍), 1011–1077, a renowned Song dynasty philosopher and cosmologist</note> wrote in the "Chant on the Winter Solstice" from his *Collection of Beating the Earth*:

-> "The Winter Solstice occurs at the midpoint of the Rat;
The Heart of Heaven remains unmoved." <-

This is indeed the case. Zhu Huian <note>original: Zhu Xi (朱熹), 1130–1200, the most influential Neo-Confucian scholar</note> considered this poem of Shao’s to be the very best explanation. 

This is because the **Start of Winter** occurs at the beginning of the tenth month; **Minor Snow** is in the middle of the tenth month; **Major Snow** is at the beginning of the eleventh month; and the **Winter Solstice** is in the middle of the eleventh month. Furthermore, **Minor Cold** occurs at the beginning of the twelfth month, and **Major Cold** is in the middle of the twelfth month. The "midpoint of the Rat" during the Winter Solstice is precisely the middle of the eleventh lunar month. 

People say that the hour of the Rat at midnight is the Winter Solstice because the half-hour before midnight belongs to the Rat hour; today, many who calculate the **Five Elements** <term>Wuxing (五行): The five phases of Wood, Fire, Earth, Metal, and Water that govern natural cycles.</term> do not understand this. Yet, every calculation must begin from this point without the slightest deviation; this is why it is called the **Heart of Heaven** <note>Tianxin (天心): The metaphysical center or pivot of the universe where the first spark of creation/Yang returns.</note>. Now, Lord Wei <note>original: Wei Boyang (魏伯陽), the legendary author of the "Seal of the Unity of the Three"</note> uses the *Book of Changes* to unify the Heart of Heaven through the Hexagram of Return.

<summary>This page defines the Hexagram of Return (Fu) as the start of the alchemical cycle, aligning it with the Winter Solstice and the "midpoint of the Rat" (midnight). It cites the authority of philosophers Shao Yong and Zhu Xi to establish that this precise moment is the "Heart of Heaven," where the first Yang energy returns.</summary>

<page-num>Fifteen</page-num>
<sig>Elucidation of the Seal of the Unity of the Three</sig>

"The construction of the hexagrams begins at the very start" refers to how the **Yang energy** <term>Yang qi (陽氣): The active, light, and masculine principle in Chinese cosmology, which in alchemy represents the spark of life or "True Lead."</term> begins to sprout latently in the month of **Early Winter** <note>The tenth month of the lunar calendar.</note>, a month of pure **Yin** <term>Yin (陰): The passive, dark, and feminine principle.</term>, beginning beneath the **Hexagram of Kun** <term>Kun (坤): The 2nd hexagram of the I Ching, consisting of six broken lines, representing pure Yin and the Earth.</term>. It gradually accumulates to form a single line of Yang, and only then does it transform into the **Hexagram of Return** <term>Fu (復): The 24th hexagram, representing the return of Yang energy (one solid line at the bottom).</term>. 

People naturally know that the tenth month corresponds to Kun, and that by the eleventh month, one Yang line appears beneath the five Yin lines to become the Hexagram of Return. However, they do not realize that at the **Winter Solstice** in the eleventh month, a single line of Yang does not simply emerge out of thin air or suddenly transform into Return without cause. 

In fact, during **Minor Snow** <note>original: Xiaoxue (小雪), a solar term occurring around November 22nd.</note> in the tenth month, the bottom line of the Kun hexagram already has Yang growing within it. But within a single day or a single month, it only grows by one-thirtieth of its total. It must accumulate for a full month until the Winter Solstice of the eleventh month before it finally fills a complete line to become the Hexagram of Return. 

Yet, this is also a metaphor. In the cycle of the year, the Winter Solstice is the "Return"; in the cycle of the month, the **New Moon morning** <term>Shuodan (朔旦): The first day of the lunar month when the moon begins its new cycle.</term> is the "Return"; and in the cycle of the day, the **hour of the Rat** <term>Zishi (子時): 11:00 PM to 1:00 AM.</term> is the "Return." All of these are merely used as examples to illuminate the cultivation of the body.

<summary>Yu Yan explains that the transition from pure Yin (Kun) to the first stage of Yang (Fu) is a gradual process of accumulation rather than a sudden leap, using the solar calendar to metaphorically describe internal alchemical refinement.</summary>
<keywords>Yang energy, Kun Hexagram, Fu Hexagram, Winter Solstice, Minor Snow, alchemical metaphor, internal cultivation</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the specific calendar-based timing of the hexagrams discussed previously to a more internal, fractal understanding of time. ->Imperially Commissioned Complete Library of the Four Treasuries<-
->Volume One<-

The internal workings of the **Great Transformation** <term>Great Transformation (造化, Zaohua): The creative and transformative power of nature; in alchemy, it refers to the process of refining the self in mimicry of cosmic cycles.</term> should not be rigidly restricted to specific years, months, days, or hours. The *Treatise of Emerald Emptiness* <note>original: "Cuixu pian" (翠虚篇), a text attributed to the Southern School Daoist master Chen Nan.</note> says: "The thirty days of a single month are the same as a single day." In the **Method of the Elixir** <term>Dan fa (丹法): The specific techniques used in internal alchemy to refine energy and spirit.</term>, one imitates the movements of nature to seize the celestial power. This is because a year is the same as a month, a month is the same as a day, and a day is the same as an hour. 

Thus, the cycle of the year is condensed into a month, the month into a day, and the day into a single hour. Within a single hour, there is naturally a mechanism for the **Return of the One Yang** <term>Yi yang lai fu (一陽來復): The first stirring of active, vital energy (Yang) within a state of total stillness (Yin). In the I Ching, this is represented by the Hexagram of Return.</term>. This mechanism is not found solely at the literal **Winter Solstice**, nor only on the first day of the month, nor only during the **Rat hour** <note>The "Zi" hour, 11 PM to 1 AM.</note>. Unless one deeply understands the **Yin and Yang** of Heaven and Earth, no one can know this profound mechanism; it is secret indeed.

## The Eldest Son Inherits the Father’s Body; the Foundation is Established through the Mother

The Father, **Qian** <term>Qian (乾): The hexagram representing Pure Yang, Heaven, and the creative principle.</term>, interacts below with the first line of the Mother, **Kun** <term>Kun (坤): The hexagram representing Pure Yin, Earth, and the receptive principle.</term>, and thereby forms **Zhen** <term>Zhen (震): The hexagram representing Thunder and the 'Eldest Son.' It consists of one Yang line at the bottom beneath two Yin lines, symbolizing the first movement of life.</term>. Zhen represents the eldest son; therefore, he is called the "Eldest..."

<summary>The author explains that alchemical timing is fractal and internal rather than literal, meaning the "Return of the Yang" can be achieved within a single hour. The text then introduces the Zhen hexagram as the first offspring of the union between Heaven (Qian) and Earth (Kun).</summary>
<keywords>fractal time, Emerald Emptiness, One Yang, Zhen hexagram, Qian and Kun, internal alchemy, Great Transformation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the argument that alchemical timing is internal rather than external. It uses the trigrams of the I Ching to illustrate how the first movement of life energy (Zhen/The Eldest Son) is born from the receptive potential of the body (Kun/The Mother) through the influence of the spirit (Qian/The Father).</meta>

<margin>
The *Seal of the Unity of the Three* according to the *Book of Changes*, Divided into Chapters
</margin>

The son succeeds the father’s essence; the trigram **Zhen** <note>The trigram ☳, representing Thunder and the first stage of returning Yang energy.</note> is born from the body of **Kun** <note>The trigram ☷, representing Earth and the pure receptive Yin.</note>, much like an infant grows within a mother’s womb. Thus, it is said: “Establish the foundation of the omen through the mother.” <note>original: "因母立兆基" (yin mu li zhao ji). This refers to the alchemical principle that the "Golden Seed" must be planted in the "Earth" of the lower abdomen/dantian.</note> 

The *Chapters on Awakening to Reality* <note>original: "Wuzhen pian" (悟真篇), a foundational 11th-century text on internal alchemy by Zhang Boduan.</note> states: “The **Golden Lord** <term>Jin Gong (金公): A technical term for 'True Lead' or the primordial breath (Yuanqi). The character for 'Lead' (鉛) is composed of 'Gold' (金) and 'Lord' (公).</term> was originally the son of the household in the East, yet was sent to the neighbor in the West to be born and succeed the lineage.” 

This passage shares the same underlying principle. By understanding this, one realizes that **refining the Elixir** <term>Lian dan (煉丹): The process of internal cultivation where the practitioner's internal energies are "cooked" and refined.</term> does not require waiting for the literal **Winter Solstice** <note>The shortest day of the year, traditionally seen as the moment Yang begins to return to the world.</note>. Within the practitioner's own body, the **Single Yang** <note>The first spark of vital energy.</note> arises of its own accord. One should not be rigidly bound by literal interpretations of years, months, days, or hours.

<note>A vertical diagram illustrates the cosmological and alchemical birth of the "Eldest Son" (the Zhen trigram). At the top right, a circle represents the **Sun**, labeled "Father" alongside the trigram **Qian** (☰, representing pure Heaven/Spirit). At the top left, a dark circle represents the **Moon**, labeled "Mother" alongside the trigram **Kun** (☷, representing pure Earth/Body). Lines from both parents converge downward toward a large arc, symbolizing the womb or the vessel. Beneath this arc, the "Eldest Son" is shown, represented by the trigram **Zhen** (☳, symbolizing the first line of Yang entering the Yin).</note>

# Waxing and Waning Respond to the Musical Tones; Rising and Falling Rely on the Pivot of the Dipper

From the hour of the **Rat** to the hour of the **Snake**, energy is in a state of **waxing** <note>The hours from 11:00 PM to 11:00 AM, associated with the growth of Yang energy.</note>. From the hour of the **Horse** to the hour of the **Pig**, energy is in a state of **waning** <note>The hours from 11:00 AM to 11:00 PM, associated with the growth of Yin energy.</note>. 

That "waxing and waning respond to the musical tones" means that within the twelve months of a year, the **musical tones** <term>Zhonglü (鍾律): The ancient Chinese system of twelve pitch-pipes, each corresponding to a month, used here to symbolize the stages of energetic development.</term> change by one pipe each month until all twelve tones have been cycled through in a year. The "waxing and waning" of the **Fire Phasing** <term>Huohou (火候): The "heat" or intensity of internal cultivation, which must be adjusted according to a precise schedule, similar to the "cooking" process of an elixir.</term> within our own bodies operates in exactly the same way.

To move from the bottom upward is called "rising"; to move from the top downward is called "falling." That "rising and falling rely on the pivot of the Dipper" means that within the twelve hours of a single day, the **pivot of the Dipper** <note>The handle of the Big Dipper (Ursa Major), which was used as a celestial clock in ancient China.</note> shifts its position every hour, moving through all twelve **Earthly Branches** <term>Dizhi (地支): The twelve-fold cycle used to mark time. Each "hour" in this system represents two modern hours.</term> in the course of one day. The "rising and falling" of the Fire Phasing within our own bodies operates in exactly the same way.

The *Chapters on Pointing to the Mysterious* <note>original: "Zhi Xuan Pian" (指玄篇), a classic text of internal alchemy often attributed to the immortal Lü Dongbin.</note> says: 
"Silently, the nine lands shift the musical pipes; 
Darkly, the vast heavens revolve the handle of the Dipper."

These are merely symbols and metaphors used to illustrate the process; it does not mean one literally uses the length of the musical pitch-pipes or the [position] of the **Pole Star** <note>original: "Tiangang" (天罡), referring to the stars of the Big Dipper or the celestial pivot.</note>...

<summary>This page explains how the internal "Fire Phasing" of alchemy mirrors natural cycles: the monthly progression of musical pitch-pipes and the hourly movement of the Big Dipper's handle.</summary>
<keywords>Fire Phasing (Huohou), Musical Tones (Zhonglü), Big Dipper, Earthly Branches, Internal Alchemy, Waxing and Waning</keywords>

### On the third day, the moon emerges at the Geng position; the Zhen trigram receives Geng in the West.

On the third day of the lunar month, the **light is newly born** <term>Zai sheng ming (哉生明): A classical phrase from the *Book of Documents* referring to the first appearance of the crescent moon.</term>. At dusk, the moon is visible in the Western position of **Geng** <note>original: "Geng" (庚). In the system of the Ten Celestial Stems, Geng is associated with the West and the element of Metal.</note>. Its image resembles the **Zhen** trigram <note>The "Thunder" trigram (☳), consisting of one solid Yang line beneath two broken Yin lines, representing the first movement of life and energy.</note>, responding to the way the **Zhen** trigram incorporates the Geng position. 

If we speak of this in terms of the **Fire Phasing** <term>Huohou (火候): The precise timing and regulation of internal heat and intention during meditation.</term> within our own bodies, then this is the specific time when the **River Chariot** <term>Heche (河車): An alchemical metaphor for the circulation of internal energy (Qi) through the "Microcosmic Orbit," specifically the ascent of energy up the spine. Like a waterwheel, it must remain in constant motion.</term> "dares not stop or linger for even a moment."

<note>A diagram illustrates the lunar phases and alchemical correlations. At the top is a horizontal crescent moon with its points facing upwards, symbolizing the thin sliver of the new moon. Directly below the moon is the trigram Zhen (☳), which represents the first stirrings of Yang energy within the body. Below the trigram is the Chinese character 'Geng' (庚), which signifies the West and the beginning of the metal phase in the alchemical cycle.</note>

<summary>This page explains the symbolic alignment of the third day of the moon with the Zhen trigram and the Western direction. It links these celestial observations to the "River Chariot" phase of internal alchemy, where energy must circulate without interruption.</summary>
<keywords>Zhen trigram, Geng (Western position), River Chariot, Fire Phasing, lunar alchemy, internal circulation, Yu Yan</keywords>

# On the eighth day, the Dui trigram ☱ receives Ding; the upper quarter is level as a string.

When the moon reaches the eighth day, it is the **upper quarter** <term>Shangxian (上弦): Literally "the upper string." This refers to the first quarter moon, where the straight edge of the half-illuminated disk resembles a bowstring.</term>. At dusk, it is situated in the **southern position of Ding** <note>original: "Ding" (丁). In the system of the Ten Celestial Stems, Ding is associated with the South and the element of Fire.</note>. In alchemy, this represents the point where Yang energy has grown to equal Yin.</note>, responding to the way the Dui trigram incorporates the celestial stem Ding. This serves as a metaphor for the halfway point of the **Yang fire** <term>Yanghuo (陽火): The active, heating energy used in the alchemical process to "cook" the internal elixir.</term> as it rises within our own bodies.

## By Imperial Order: Complete Library of the Four Treasuries, Volume One

As stated in the <term>Yellow Court Scripture<note>The *Huangting jing*, a seminal Daoist text focused on visualizing the deities and energies within the human body’s internal organs.</note></term>: "The sun rises and the moon sets; the breath is preserved." This is because the cycle of breathing is, in essence, the movement of the sun and the moon.

-> The toad follows the rhythm of the hexagrams; the rabbit breathes out the light of birth. <-

In <term>alchemical practice<note>Specifically referring to *Neidan* (Inner Alchemy), where the practitioner’s own body and breath are used as the laboratory to cultivate immortality.</note></term>, the first half of the lunar month is considered Yang, corresponding to the hexagrams **Zhen** (Thunder), **Dui** (Lake), and **Qian** (Heaven). The second half of the month is Yin, corresponding to the hexagrams **Xun** (Wind), **Gen** (Mountain), and **Kun** (Earth). Thus, it is said that "the toad follows the rhythm of the hexagrams." The moon is the essence of Great Yin, while the sun is the essence of Great Yang. Yang governs the act of "spitting out" (emission), and Yin governs "receiving." The moon possesses no light of its own; it receives the sun’s light and becomes white. This is why it is said that "the rabbit breathes out the light of birth." <note>In Chinese mythology, the toad and the rabbit are residents of the moon; here they symbolize the lunar phases used to time alchemical internal work.</note>

-> When the path of seven and eight is complete, it bends and descends low. <-

"Seven and eight" refers to the fifteenth day of the month <note>7 + 8 = 15, the night of the full moon.</note>. The <term>Yang fire<note>Yang fire (*yanghuo*) refers to the active, rising phase of internal energy circulation during meditation.</note></term> rises from the stage of **Zhen** until it reaches the fifteenth day, which is pure **Qian** (Full Heaven). At this point, the time-period of the first half of the month is full. Its momentum has reached its absolute peak, and its path has become precarious. Therefore, the energy cannot help but begin to bend and curve downward.

<summary>This page explains the astronomical and calendrical metaphors used in Inner Alchemy, specifically how the lunar cycle and hexagrams dictate the timing of breath and energy circulation.</summary>
<keywords>Yellow Court Scripture, lunar phases, hexagrams, Yang fire, Zhen, Qian, toad and rabbit symbols, internal alchemy timing</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the lunar cycle as a metaphor for internal alchemy, transitioning from the peak of Yang energy (the 15th day/Full Moon) to the beginning of the Yin phase (the 16th day).</meta>

...it turns, revolves, and descends low. Wang Zhi <note>Also known by the Daoist name Chaoyuan, a figure from the Chen dynasty (557–589 CE).</note> wrote in his book: "Within the nine-layered city, the dragon-chariot departs; before the twelve towers, the tiger-carriage returns." This is exactly what is meant here. <note>The "nine-layered city" and "twelve towers" are traditional alchemical metaphors for the head and the throat or internal organs, describing the circular path of energy moving through the body.</note>

### On the sixteenth day, the cycle turns and receives the command; the trigram **Xun** and the stem **Xin** appear at dawn.

When the moon reaches the sixteenth day, the <term>waning soul<note>The term *jishengpo* refers to the beginning of the moon's dark phase; as the light decreases, the "soul" or shadow of the moon grows.</note></term> has already begun to emerge. In the morning, the moon is positioned in the West, at the station of the celestial stem **Xin**. Its brightness suddenly diminishes, and its appearance resembles the trigram **Xun** <note>**Xun** (Wind) consists of two solid Yang lines above a broken Yin line, representing the first "leak" or entry of Yin into a previously pure Yang state.</note>. This corresponds to the trigram **Xun**’s association with the West and the stem **Xin**. 

If we speak of this in terms of the <term>internal fire<note>Internal fire (*huoshi*) refers to the conscious direction of energy and heat during meditation to transform the body's essence.</note></term> within our own bodies, this is the moment when the Yin energy receives the "abdication" of the Yang energy. It is the turning point where the path winds and the peaks shift. <note>The phrase "the path winds and the peaks turn" (*fenghui luzhuan*) illustrates a major transition or a change in direction in the alchemical process.</note>

<summary>This page describes the transition of internal energy on the sixteenth day of the alchemical cycle, where the peak Yang energy begins to give way to Yin, symbolized by the trigram Xun and the moon's waning.</summary>

### 3. Gen Aligns with Bing in the South: The Last Quarter on the Twenty-Third Day

When the moon reaches the twenty-third day, it is the <term>Last Quarter<note>Original: *xiaxian* (下弦), literally "lower bowstring." This refers to the phase where the moon's straight edge appears like a bowstring, occurring as the moon wanes toward the new moon.</note></term>. At dawn, it is positioned in the South, at the station of the celestial stem **Bing**. At this time, its brightness is half-diminished, and its appearance is like the trigram **Gen** <note>The **Gen** (Mountain) trigram consists of one solid Yang line at the top over two broken Yin lines below. This visualizes the "top" half of the moon still retaining some light while the bottom has darkened.</note>. This corresponds to the trigram **Gen**’s association with the stem **Bing** in the <term>Najia system<note>A system of symbolic correlations that assigns the eight trigrams to the ten celestial stems to track the movement of energy through time and space.</note></term>. 

In the context of the internal work, this is used as a metaphor for the halfway point of the <term>Yin Talisman’s<note>The "Yin Talisman" (*yinfu*) refers to the "cooling" or "withdrawing" phase of the alchemical process. After the peak of energy (the Full Moon), the practitioner begins a controlled descent of the internal breath to settle and store the refined elixir.</note></term> descent within our own bodies.

### When the Periods End, Succession Occurs: The Return and Rebirth of the Dragon

Master Wei <note>Master Wei refers to Wei Boyang (魏伯陽), the legendary 2nd-century author of the *Seal of the Unity of the Three*, the foundational text of internal alchemy.</note> divided the thirty days of a single month into six distinct periods. 

From the dawn of the new moon until the fifth day is the **first period**, which belongs to the trigram **Zhen** <note>Original: *Zhen* (震). Known as "The Arousing" or Thunder, it represents the first appearance of light (Yang) in the lunar cycle.</note>. 

From the sixth day to the tenth day is the **second period**, which belongs to the trigram **Dui** <note>Original: *Dui* (兌). Known as "The Joyous" or Lake, it represents the moon as it approaches the half-way point of its waxing phase.</note>. 

From the eleventh day to the fifteenth day is the **third period**, which belongs to the trigram **Qian** <note>Original: *Qian* (乾). Known as "The Creative" or Heaven, it represents the full moon and the peak of Yang energy.</note>. 

From the sixteenth day to the twentieth day is the **fourth period**, which belongs to the trigram **Xun** <note>Original: *Xun* (巽). Known as "The Gentle" or Wind, it represents the first sliver of darkness (Yin) appearing as the moon begins to wane.</note>. 

From the twenty-first day to the twenty-fifth day is the **fifth period**, which belongs to the trigram **Gen** <note>Original: *Gen* (艮). Known as "Keeping Still" or Mountain, it represents the moon in its final quarter, where light is almost gone.</note>. 

From the twenty-sixth day [to the thirtieth day is the final period]...

<column-break/>

This corresponds to the trigram **Kun**'s association with the celestial stem **Yi** <note>In the *Najia* (Matching Stems) system of correlations, the trigram Kun (Earth/Pure Yin) is assigned to the stems Yi and Gui to represent the end of the light cycle and the total dominance of Yin.</note>, serving as a metaphor for the moment when the <term>Yin Talisman<note>Original: *yinfu* (陰符). In internal alchemy, the "Yin Talisman" refers to the "cooling" or "withdrawing" phase of the meditative process, following the peak of the "Yang Fire."</note></term> within one's own body has reached its absolute conclusion.

<column-break/>

When the moon reaches the thirtieth day, it enters the phase called <term>Hui<note>Original: *hui* (晦). This refers to the "dark moon" or the very end of the lunar month when the moon is no longer visible. In alchemical practice, it represents a state of total stillness and the "void" before a new cycle of energy is born.</note></term>. At dawn, it is positioned in the East at the station of the celestial stem **Yi**. Its light is completely exhausted and lost; its symbolic image is like that of the trigram **Kun** (☷).

<column-break/>

# Moon

### The Sixth Period: Completion and the Rebirth of the Dragon

By the **thirtieth day**, we reach the **sixth period**, which belongs to the trigram **Kun** <note>Original: *Kun* (坤). Representing the "Receptive" or Earth, it consists of three broken lines, signifying pure Yin or total darkness.</note>. Once these six periods are completed—following the **conjunction of the sun and moon** <note>The "conjunction" (*heshuo*) refers to the new moon, when the sun and moon are aligned from the perspective of Earth.</note>—the Yang energy once again receives the "abdication" from the Yin. It transforms back into the trigram **Zhen**. 

**Zhen** is the **Dragon**; it represents a single Yang line moving beneath two Yin lines (☳). Below the weight of the "heavy abyss," there is a living creature in motion—is this not indeed the **Dragon**? <note>In internal alchemy, the "Dragon" represents the initial spark of life or the "True Lead" emerging from the depths of meditative stillness.</note>

->**The Stems Ren and Gui pair with Jia and Yi;**<-
->**Qian and Kun encompass the beginning and the end.**<-

The trigram **Qian** <term>Qian (乾)|The "Creative" or Heaven trigram, representing pure Yang energy.</term> incorporates the celestial stems **Jia** and **Ren**, while the trigram **Kun** <term>Kun (坤)|The "Receptive" or Earth trigram, representing pure Yin energy.</term> incorporates **Yi** and **Gui**. Therefore, it is said that "Ren and Gui pair with Jia and Yi." The **Ten Celestial Stems** <note>A traditional Chinese system of cyclical signs used for timekeeping, directions, and alchemy.</note> begin with Jia and Yi and conclude with Ren and Gui; hence it is said that "**Qian** and **Kun** encompass the beginning and the end." 

Generally, the system of **matching stems** <note>Original: *Najia* (納甲). An esoteric system of mapping the eight trigrams to the ten celestial stems to track the movement of energy through time.</note> is a symbolic representation of the **fire phasing** <note>Original: *Huohou* (火候). This refers to the timing and intensity of the alchemical "fire"—the practitioner's meditative focus and breath—during the refinement of the internal elixir.</note>. The process of "adding and subtracting" during fire phasing is no different from the waxing and waning of the moon.

If we now distribute the six trigrams across a single month:
*   The image of **Zhen** <note>The Arousing/Thunder (☳)</note> appears on the **third day**, with the moon emerging in the direction of the stem **Geng** <note>The West.</note>.
*   The image of **Dui** <note>The Joyous/Lake (☱)</note> represents the **first quarter moon**, appearing in the direction of the stem **Ding** <note>The South.</note>.
*   The image of **Qian**...

<summary>This page explains the final stage of the lunar cycle (the 30th day) and the subsequent rebirth of Yang energy, represented by the trigram Zhen and the image of a dragon. It introduces the "Matching Stems" (Najia) system to explain how alchemical "fire phasing" mirrors the moon's phases.</summary>

The author, Yu Yan, clarifies that these astronomical observations are intended as alchemical metaphors for the internal process rather than literal calendar instructions.</meta>

# Imperial Edition of the Complete Library in Four Sections, Volume One

### The Symbolic Lunar Cycle and the Fire Phasing

The image of the **Full Moon** corresponds to the sun and moon meeting in the position of **Jia** <note>The first of the Ten Celestial Stems, associated here with the direction East and the peak of Yang energy.</note>. On the **sixteenth day**, the image of the trigram **Xun** ☴ appears, as the moon begins to wane at the position of **Xin**. At the **Last Quarter**, the image of the trigram **Gen** ☶ appears, as the moon’s light fades at the position of **丙** (Bing). During the **Dark Moon**, the image of the trigram **Kun** ☷ appears, and the sun and moon both "set" or disappear at the position of **Yi**. 

However, these descriptions are merely **borrowed comparisons** used to discuss the **advancement and retreat** of the **Fire Phases** <term>Fire Phasing (火候, huohou)|The precise timing and intensity of meditative focus and internal heat required to "cook" the alchemical elixir within the body.</term> of the **six trigrams** within the practitioner's body. One should not truly believe this refers to a literal month of thirty days. 

How can we prove this? If we consult the laws of the calendar, we see that the length of day and night varies. If the day is short and the sun sets in the direction of **Shen** <note>One of the twelve Earthly Branches, associated with late afternoon (3-5 PM) and the southwest.</note>, then the moon conjoins in **Shen** and is full in **Yin** <note>The direction of the northeast.</note>. If the day is long and the sun sets in **Xu** <note>Early evening (7-9 PM) and the northwest.</note>, then the moon conjoins in **Xu** and is full in **Chen** <note>The direction of the southeast.</note>. 

Throughout the twelve months of the year, the moon of the **third day** might not always be visible exactly at the position of **Geng**. The moon of the **fifteenth day** might not always be seen exactly at **Jia**. Because the **conjunction of the sun and moon** <note>Original: *heshuo* (合朔), the astronomical New Moon.</note> can occur earlier or later, the **First and Last Quarters** do not necessarily fall exactly on the eighth and twenty-third days. Similarly, the **Full Moon** and the **Dark Moon** do not always fall exactly on the fifteenth and thirtieth days. 

Now, **Lord Wei** <note>Refers to Wei Boyang (魏伯陽), the legendary author of the *Cantong Qi* (The Seal of the Unity of the Three).</note> states that the three...

<summary>Yu Yan explains that the correlation between lunar phases, trigrams, and celestial stems is a metaphorical framework for internal alchemy (Fire Phasing) rather than literal astronomy. He argues that because actual lunar cycles vary in timing, the text's specific dates should be understood as symbolic markers for the practitioner's internal energy shifts.</summary>

When the sun rises, it corresponds to the trigram **Dui** ☱. The trigram **Zhen** ☳ receives the light at the position of **Geng** <note>The seventh Celestial Stem, associated with the West. In alchemical theory, this represents the first appearance of light/Yang.</note> in the western direction. On the **sixteenth day**, the cycle turns and is governed by **Xun** ☴; the position of **Xin** <note>The eighth Celestial Stem, also associated with the West. Here it represents the first arrival of darkness/Yin.</note> becomes visible at the first light of dawn. 

These descriptions generally refer to the **second and eighth months** <note>The spring and autumn equinoxes, when the balance of day and night is perfect.</note>, when day and night are of equal length. They are merely **borrowed images** used to illustrate a point; it does not truly mean that one must **advance the fire** <term>Advancing the Fire (進火, jinhuo)|The active phase of meditation where the practitioner focuses on "heating" and circulating the internal energy (Yang) to forge the elixir.</term> at the specific moment the moon appears at Geng, nor **retreat the tally** <term>Retreating the Tally (退符, tuifu)|The passive or "cooling" phase of meditation where the practitioner allows Yin energy to descend and the internal state to stabilize, preventing exhaustion.</term> when the moon begins to wane at the position of Xin. 

The scholar should simply observe the **waxing and waning** of the moon’s body and seek those principles reflected back within their own person:

*   The birth of **one Yang** within the body is the image of **Zhen** ☳ <note>Represented by a single solid line at the bottom: ☳.</note>, corresponding to the new moon’s birth on the **third day**. 
*   The growth of **two Yang** is the image of **Dui** ☱ <note>Represented by two solid lines: ☱.</note>, corresponding to the upper-quarter moon on the **eighth day**. 
*   The fullness of **three Yang** is the image of **Qian** ☰ <note>Three solid lines, representing pure Yang: ☰.</note>, corresponding to the roundness of the full moon on the **fifteenth day**. 
*   The birth of **one Yin** is the image of **Xun** ☴ <note>Represented by a single broken line at the bottom: ☴.</note>, corresponding to the moon beginning to wane on the **sixteenth day**. 
*   The growth of **two Yin** is the image of **Gen** ☶ <note>Represented by two broken lines: ☶.</note>, corresponding to the lower-quarter moon on the **twenty-third day**. 
*   The completion of **three Yin** is the image of **Kun** ☷ <note>Three broken lines, representing pure Yin and total receptivity: ☷.</note>, corresponding to the moon disappearing on the **thirtieth day**. 

How could one be restricted by the literal directions of the moon’s rising and setting...?

# Seal of the Unity of the Three according to the *Book of Changes*

The Way of Heaven is exceedingly vast and wide. The true mechanism <note>original: 真機 (zhenji); the hidden pivot or secret trigger of natural processes</note> exists within a mere instant. The Great Mystery is without form; the location of its wondrous subtlety lies within the profound darkness <note>original: 窈冥 (yaoming); a state of deep, dark, and silent potentiality described in the Tao Te Ching</note>.

When this happens, the Spirit congeals and the Breath gathers; the Golden Liquid <note>The refined elixir of immortality</note> then crystallizes. Should one forget this commitment to silence and allow the breath to rush out through the Twelve-Storied Tower <note>original: 重樓 (zhonglou); a poetic term for the throat</note>, one brings about their own injury and failure.

<meta>Volume One</meta>

The count of ten days is entirely established as a set of symbols and metaphors. Lord Wei <note>original: 魏公 (Wei Gong); referring to Wei Boyang, the legendary 2nd-century author of the *Seal of the Unity of the Three*</note> feared that scholars would fail to grasp his explanation and, like the blind leading the blind, would mistakenly assume that the waxing, waning, growth, and decay <note>original: 盈虚消息 (yingxu xiaoxi); terms from the *Book of Changes* describing the cyclic nature of Yin and Yang</note> of thirty days constituted a literal monthly "Fire Phasing" <term>Fire Phasing<note>original: 火候 (huohou); the precise timing and intensity of internal energetic cultivation</note></term>. 

Therefore, he separately ordered these "Four Images" of Seven, Eight, Nine, and Six <note>In the *Book of Changes*, these numbers represent Young Yang (7), Young Yin (8), Old Yang (9), and Old Yin (6)</note> to enlighten those who have not yet understood. This was done to manifest the truth: that the counts of thirty days in a month are all metaphors and are not literal. 

The practice of "advancing the fire" on the third day of the month and "withdrawing the talismans" <note>Stopping the active circulation of breath/energy</note> on the sixteenth day is the same. It is similar to the use of the cardinal directions <note>original: 子午卯酉 (Zi, Wu, Mao, You); North/Midnight, South/Noon, East/Sunrise, and West/Sunset</note>, the earthly branches <note>original: 子申寅戌 (Zi, Shen, Yin, Xu)</note>, the four seasons, the equinoxes and solstices, the openings and closings, day and night, or dawn and dusk. All of these—returning, reversing, and dwelling—share this same underlying meaning. 

If scholars recognize that Seven, Eight, Nine, and Six are identical to the count of thirty days in a month, then all other broad references and winding metaphors can be understood through silent realization. Why must one cling so rigidly to the literal text or get bogged down by the symbols <note>original: 泥象 (nixiang); to be "stuck in the mud" of external forms or imagery</note>?

### The Eight Trigrams are Arranged as the Luminaries Move Without Losing the Center

The "Center" refers to the Yellow Path <term>Yellow Path<note>original: 黃道 (huangdao); astronomically the ecliptic, but in internal alchemy, it refers to the central, balanced pathway of energy within the body.</note></term>. The Eight Trigrams are distributed across the eight directions, and the Twenty-Eight Mansions <note>The constellations of the Chinese zodiac</note> are arranged in a circle around them; this is the road upon which the Sun and Moon travel back and forth. 

The *Chapters on Awakening to Perfection* <note>original: 悟真篇 (Wuzhen pian); a foundational 11th-century text on internal alchemy</note> says: "Once the two things are driven back to the Yellow Path, how could the Numinous Elixir fail to be born?" When refining the Elixir, the only fear is that the "Mind-Monkey" <note>original: 心猿 (xinyuan); a metaphor for the restless, jumping nature of the human mind that is difficult to control</note> will escape and run wild on the outside. 

If one is able to "withdraw the vision and return the hearing" <note>A practice of turning the senses inward away from the external world</note> to concentrate the spirit for even a brief moment—causing the "two things" <note>referring to the internal Yin and Yang, or "Lead" and "Mercury"</note> to return to the Yellow Path without losing their way from the center—then the enveloping mists of primordial vapor will intermingle and copulate. They will condense into a single drop of "dew," which then flies down into the Cinnabar Field <term>Cinnabar Field<note>original: 丹田 (dantian); the energetic center in the lower abdomen where the elixir is gathered and stored.</note></term>.

### Primordial Essence is Subtle and Difficult to Behold; Infer and Measure to Verify the Signs

The *Classic of the Way and Virtue* <note>original: 道德經 (Daodejing); referring to Chapter 21</note> says: "Deep and obscure! Within it is the Essence."

### When Dwelling, One Observes Its Images and Models Its Form

When refining the elixir, one should externally set up gnomons and water clocks <term>Water Clock<note>original: 刻漏 (kelou); an ancient clepsydra used to measure time by the flow of water.</note></term> to measure the precise intervals of the heavenly movements. As soon as a distinct root is established, Heaven differentiates **Creation** <term>Creation<note>original: 造化 (zaohua); the spontaneous and creative transformative power of nature and the universe.</note> from within the **One** <note>original: 一 (yi); the primordial unity or the Dao from which all things emerge.</note>. How can there be two separate meanings for Heaven and Man? The **Dao** <note>The Way; the fundamental principle of the universe.</note> does not act in vain; it resides solely in Man. How profound is its purpose!

When in **motion**, it follows the transformations of the **Hexagrams** <term>Hexagrams<note>original: 卦 (gua); the sixty-four symbols of the I Ching used to map the flow of cosmic time and change.</note>; when in **stillness**, it adheres to the **Judgment** <term>Judgment<note>original: 彖辭 (tuanci); the specific portion of the I Ching text that explains the core meaning and internal logic of a hexagram.</note>.

While the myriad things act together, I observe their **return**." Now, the myriad things are flourishing, yet each returns to its root. How supreme! This is how the myriad things return to their destiny.

The myriad things owe their birth to it, for it submissively receives the influence of Heaven.” The Receptive is thick and carries all things; its virtue aligns with the boundless. Furthermore, **Kun** <note>The hexagram representing Earth.</note> represents the Earth, the essence of **pure Yin**...

...the **Hexagram** <term>Hexagram<note>original: 卦 (gua); the sixty-four symbols of the I Ching. This refers to Laozi’s description of the space between Heaven and Earth acting like a bellows—empty yet inexhaustible.</note> to receive the breath of Heaven. Consequently, all the "hundred flourishings" <note>A poetic term for the myriad living things and plants.</note> are produced from the soil. 

In the **Alchemical Method** <term>Alchemical Method<note>original: 丹法 (danfa); the specific internal practices used to cultivate the "Golden Elixir" within the body.</note>, when one reaches the stage of **containing the light and reflecting inward** <note>original: 含光返照 (hanguang fanzhao); a meditative state where the practitioner withdraws their sensory attention from the outside world and focuses their internal "light" or consciousness onto the inner self.</note>, the spirit is hidden deep within. This state is no different from the months of **Pure Yin** <note>The period of the lunar calendar associated with early winter, when the life force of nature is hidden and dormant.</note>, when the world is closed off to form winter.

The *Chapter on Returning to Life* <note>original: 復命篇 (Fuming Pian); a classic text of internal alchemy often attributed to the Tang dynasty adept Xue Daoguang.</note> says: 

> "Having received the secret formula of the true immortals, one refines the **Ultimate Yang** from within the **Yin**."

This is exactly what is meant here. However, when stillness reaches its peak, **movement** must necessarily follow. In an instant, the **One Yang** <note>The first spark of active, creative energy.</note> moves beneath the six Yin lines. 

Through the first transformation, it becomes the hexagram **Return** <term>Return<note>original: 復 (Fu); Hexagram 24, representing the winter solstice and the first return of light.</note>. With the second transformation, it becomes **Approach** <term>Approach<note>original: 臨 (Lin); Hexagram 19.</note>. The third becomes **Peace** <term>Peace<note>original: 泰 (Tai); Hexagram 11, where Heaven and Earth meet.</note>. The fourth becomes **Great Power** <term>Great Power<note>original: 大壯 (Da Zhuang); Hexagram 34.</note>. The fifth becomes **Breakthrough** <term>Breakthrough<note>original: 夬 (Guai); Hexagram 43.</note>. Finally, at the sixth transformation, it becomes **Pure Heaven** <term>Pure Heaven<note>original: 純乾 (Chun Qian); Hexagram 1, consisting of six solid Yang lines.</note>. 

At this point, the **Yang energy** <note>original: 陽氣 (yangqi); the active, bright, and masculine principle of life.</note> circulates entirely through the **six voids** <note>original: 六虛 (liuxu); the six positions of a hexagram, representing the totality of space/the body.</note>, and the "single wheel of the sun" appears reflected in the deep pool. <note>This "sun in the deep pool" is a common alchemical metaphor for the realization of the Golden Elixir or the "Inner Light" manifesting within the stillness of the lower abdomen (the Dantian).</note>

# Volume One

The function of **Heaven and Earth** <term>Heaven and Earth<note>original: 乾坤 (Qian and Kun); the first two hexagrams of the I Ching, representing the fundamental principles of active Yang and receptive Yin.</note> is found in **Water and Fire** <term>Water and Fire<note>original: 坎離 (Kan and Li); hexagrams representing the "functional" energies that interact within the body to produce the internal elixir.</note>. Heaven and Earth utilize Water and Fire to operate within them; because of this, Yin and Yang interact in **Harmony** <note>original: 泰 (Tai); referring to Hexagram 11 (Peace/Pervading), where the energies of Heaven and Earth meet.</note>, and a balanced vital energy brings about auspiciousness. If Heaven and Earth were without Water and Fire for even a single day, the **Creative Process** <note>original: 造化 (zaohua); the natural cycle of birth, growth, and transformation in the universe.</note> would almost certainly be extinguished. Does the wondrous method of the **Great Elixir** <note>original: 大丹 (dadan); the "Golden Elixir" of immortality sought through internal cultivation.</note>, which follows the model of Heaven and Earth, have anything different from the way Heaven and Earth themselves operate?

### The Beginning of Governing
How could one not be cautious? When the **First Yang** <note>original: 一陽 (yi yang); the first spark of returning life-energy, often associated with the winter solstice in the macrocosm and the first stirrings of vitality in the microcosm of the body.</note> just begins to move, it is the time for making the elixir. This is like the very beginning of a **sovereign's governance** <note>Yu Yan uses the metaphor of an emperor to describe the practitioner’s mind/will controlling the internal state.</note>. In issuing commands and decrees, how could one not be cautious?

->**Constraining and gathering with subtle density; secretly unfolding and spreading substance.**<-

"Constraining and gathering with subtle density" means: the eyes contain their light, the ears condense their resonance, the nose regulates its breath, and the tongue seals its... <note>The text likely continues to "seals its palate" or "speech," referring to the "Four Gates" of the senses that must be closed during meditation to prevent energy from leaking out.</note>

With breath collected and legs folded, sit upright. Submerge your spirit and **guard the interior** <term>guard the interior<note>original: 內守 (neishou); a fundamental meditative practice of focusing awareness inward to prevent the dissipation of vital energy.</note>. You must not allow even a hair’s breadth of your mind to be directed outward. 

For when the eyes do not look, the **Ethereal Soul** <term>Ethereal Soul<note>original: 魂 (hun); the celestial or "yang" aspect of the soul associated with the liver and the element of Wood.</note> naturally returns to the liver. When the ears do not hear, the **Essence** <term>Essence<note>original: 精 (jing); the foundational generative energy associated with the kidneys and the element of Water.</note> naturally returns to the kidneys. When the tongue makes no sound, the **Spirit** <term>Spirit<note>original: 神 (shen); the conscious, divine spark associated with the heart and the element of Fire.</note> naturally returns to the heart. When the nose smells no fragrance, the **Corporeal Soul** <term>Corporeal Soul<note>original: 魄 (po); the earthly or "yin" aspect of the soul associated with the lungs and the element of Metal.</note> naturally returns to the lungs. When the four limbs do not move, the **Intention** <term>Intention<note>original: 意 (yi); the directed thought or "earth" element of the mind, which acts as the stabilizer for the other four.</note> naturally returns to the spleen.

Subsequently, the Ethereal Soul resides in the liver and does not leak through the eyes; the Corporeal Soul resides in the lungs and does not leak through the nose; the Spirit resides in the heart and does not leak through the mouth; the Essence resides in the kidneys and does not leak through the ears; and the Intention resides in the spleen and does not leak through the four limbs or the bodily orifices. 

When all five of these are without "leakage" <note>In Taoist alchemy, "leakage" (lou) refers to the loss of internal life-force through sensory engagement with the external world.</note>, then the Essence, Spirit, Ethereal Soul, Corporeal Soul, and Intention blend and fuse with one another. They transform into a **Single Breath** <note>original: 一氣 (yi qi); the primordial, unified energy that precedes the division into specific elemental functions.</note> and gather in the **Cinnabar Field** <term>Cinnabar Field<note>original: 丹田 (dantian); specifically the lower Cinnabar Field, the energetic center in the lower abdomen where the elixir is forged.</note>. 

Wait until a single breath shifts at the nose and the mind becomes suddenly radiant; then the **Dragon and Tiger** <term>Dragon and Tiger<note>original: 龍虎 (longhu); symbols for the internal energies of Wood/Liver (Dragon) and Metal/Lungs (Tiger). Their "charging through the passes" signifies the successful movement of refined energy through the body's meridian gates.</note> charge through the passes. The great road is opened, and a single channel of **Jasper Nectar** <term>Jasper Nectar<note>original: 瓊漿 (qiongjiang); a poetic term for the refined alchemical fluid or "sweet dew" generated during deep meditation.</note> flows and circulates within.

# The Essential Path: The Dipper’s Handle as the Mainstay of Transformation

The essence of the **Alchemical Way** <term>Alchemical Way<note>original: 丹道 (dandao); the process of internal transformation to achieve spiritual perfection or "the Elixir."</note> resides entirely in the **Dipper** <note>original: 斗 (dou); referring to the Big Dipper (Ursa Major), which in Daoist cosmology acts as the celestial pivot that governs the movement of the stars and the seasons.</note>. The Dipper is the means by which the movement of **Yin and Yang** <term>Yin and Yang<note>the fundamental complementary forces of the universe and the body.</note> throughout the entire body is managed. it governs the **Ten Thousand Transformations** <note>the infinite internal processes and changes</note> within oneself, much like the main cord of a net or the central binding of a garment. 

Generally, the **Three Cinnabar Fields** <term>Three Cinnabar Fields<note>original: 三田 (santian); the three primary energy centers of the body located in the head, chest, and lower abdomen.</note> of the human body are divided into three separate locations. If one can master the mechanism of the **Dipper Handle** <term>Dipper Handle<note>original: 斗柄 (doubing); the moving part of the constellation used here as a metaphor for the practitioner's focused Intention (Yi) which directs the flow of internal energy.</note> to manage and rotate them, the **True Qi** <term>True Qi<note>original: 真氣 (zhenqi); the primordial, refined energy of the body.</note> will circulate upward and downward like the flowing revolution of the **Celestial River** <note>original: 天河 (tianhe); the Milky Way.</note>. This is what is meant by the "Essential Path." 

As the *Chapters on Returning to Life* <note>original: 復命篇 (Fuming pian); a classic work of internal alchemy.</note> states: "Drive back the Northern Dipper and turn the **Heavenly Guard** <term>Heavenly Guard<note>original: 天罡 (tiangang); the star at the tip of the Dipper's handle, considered a potent source of celestial energy and a symbol of the practitioner's focused will.</note>; with hands grasping the **Southern Stars** <note>original: 南辰 (nanchen); the Southern Dipper, which in Daoism is associated with the power of life and longevity.</note>, enter the **Bridal Chamber** <term>Bridal Chamber<note>original: 洞房 (dongfang); a metaphor for the inner sanctum of the mind or a specific energy center in the brain.</note>." This is indeed the most essential secret of all essentials.

# The Birth of Yang and the Celestial Governance of the Body

When **Yang** <term>Yang<note>The active, masculine, and creative principle of the universe and the body.</note> is born within the body, it is called the beginning of "receiving the Qi." Obtaining it is indeed easy; however, at the moment one applies the work <note>original: 下手 (xiashou); refers to the specific moment the practitioner begins the meditative or alchemical intervention.</note>, the qualities of **good fortune, misfortune, regret, and stinginess** <note>These four terms are classic descriptors from the *I Ching* (Book of Changes) used to categorize the outcomes of any action or movement.</note> arise from movement itself. One must not allow for the slightest hairsbreadth of error or deviation.

The **Five Planets** <term>Five Planets<note>original: 五緯 (wuwei); literally "The Five Latitudes," referring to Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn, which correspond to the Five Agents (Wuxing) in the body.</note> move in or out of alignment, responding to the seasons through their influence. The **Twenty-Eight Mansions** <note>original: 四七 (si qi); literally "four times seven," referring to the lunar mansions that divide the sky. Here, they represent the internal energetic map of the practitioner.</note> may clash or stray, deviating as one gazes upward or bows down. 

The **Five Agents** <term>Five Agents<note>original: 五行 (wuxing); Wood, Fire, Earth, Metal, and Water.</note> must be gathered together, and **Metal and Fire** <note>In internal alchemy, "Metal" often represents the refined breath or lead, and "Fire" represents the heat of focused consciousness.</note> must share the same furnace. This transformation rests entirely within a single thought of the practitioner to "return to the root and restore the source." If this is not achieved, the stars within the **tripod** <term>tripod<note>original: 鼎 (ding); the alchemical cauldron, a metaphor for the lower abdomen or the body as a whole where transformation occurs.</note> will all fall out of alignment, and Heaven and Earth will be separated by a vast distance.

## The Administrative Order of the Crimson Palace

The **Wenchang Palace** <note>original: 文昌 (wenchang); the "Palace of Literary Prosperity," a constellation associated with administration and the divine bureaucracy.</note> oversees the records, interrogating and censuring the high ministers and the hundred officials, each of whom governs their respective departments.

The **Treatise on Celestial Offices** in the *Records of the Grand Historian* <note>original: 史記天官書 (Shiji Tianguan Shu); a foundational astronomical text written by Sima Qian around 100 BCE.</note> states: "The ladle of the Dipper carries the *Kuang* [basket] stars; these six stars are called the Wenchang Palace." 

What we now call "Wenchang overseeing the records" refers to the **Son of Heaven** <note>Metaphor for the Spirit or the Mind.</note> residing in the **Crimson Palace** <term>Crimson Palace<note>original: 絳宮 (jianggong); a traditional name for the Heart center in Daoist anatomy.</note>, who governs the forces of **Qian and Kun** <note>The hexagrams representing pure Heaven/Yang and pure Earth/Yin.</note>. Within the human body, where can one find this so-called "Wenchang"? ...

How grand it is! All of these descriptions are nothing but metaphors. Heaven has the **Three Terraces** and **Four Assistants** <term>Three Terraces and Four Assistants<note>Stars in the Ursa Major constellation and near the North Star that serve as celestial governors.</note>, while the Elixir has the **Three Dragons** and **Four Tigers** <note>Metaphors for the volatile and grounded energies within the body that must be tamed.</note>.

To "call the Terraces and Assistants to account" means that one must not allow the **Jia and Yi** <note>original: 甲乙; the Wood element and the East.</note> to be lost, nor allow the **Geng and Xin** <note>original: 庚辛; the Metal element and the West.</note> to deviate. Just as the hundred officials and presiding ministers each govern their own departments, the practitioner follows the year’s **twenty-four solar terms** and the celestial sphere’s **twenty-eight lunar mansions**. Through these, the "fire timing" of the **tripod and furnace** <note>Metaphors for the lower abdomen and the focused intention used in meditation.</note> is secretly calculated and arranged.

From this perspective, the myriad phenomena of Heaven’s vast array are all contained within my own single body.

The Sun contains the essence of the **Five Agents** <term>Five Agents<note>Wood, Fire, Earth, Metal, and Water.</note>, and the Moon receives the discipline of the **Six Pitch-pipes** <note>original: 六律 (liulü); the standard ancient Chinese musical tuning system used here to represent cosmic harmony and timing.</note>. Five times six makes thirty degrees; once the thirty degrees are completed, the cycle begins anew <note>This refers to the thirty days of a lunar month.</note>. 

The Sun has the **Ten Celestial Stems** <note>A cycle of ten days used in the Chinese calendar.</note>: five stems are "hard" <note>Yang</note> and five stems are "soft" <note>Yin</note>. The Moon has the **Twelve Pitch-pipes**: six pipes are Yin and six pipes are Yang. In terms of the Alchemical Way, the Sun is the **Fire**. That "the Sun contains the essence of the Five Agents" refers to a single substance...

The Moon is the **Medicine** <note>The "Medicine" (yao) refers to the internal substances—Essence, Breath, and Spirit—that are refined during meditation to create the Elixir.</note>. That "the Moon receives the governance of the **Six Pitch-pipes**" <term>Six Pitch-pipes<note>original: 六律 (liuliu); these were ancient musical standards used to align the calendar with cosmic resonance. Here, they represent the mathematical precision of the alchemical process.</note> means that **White Gold** <note>original: 白金 (baijin); a symbolic name for the "True Lead" or the primordial spark of energy recovered from within.</note> refines the **Six Trigrams** <note>The six "moving" trigrams of the I Ching (excluding Qian and Kun) which govern the cycles of growth and decay.</note>.

"Five times six is thirty degrees." When these degrees are completed, the cycle begins again. The **Sun** <note>original: 太陽 (taiyang); representing the active, masculine Yang energy.</note> travels one degree each day. When it reaches thirty degrees, it meets again in conjunction with the **Moon** <note>original: 太陰 (taiyin); representing the receptive, feminine Yin energy.</note>. This cycle restarts and repeats, never pausing for even a moment.

### Trace the beginning and seek the end; this is the thread of survival and perishability.

The "beginning" is the **New Moon** <note>original: 朔 (shuo); the first day of the lunar month.</note>. The "end" is the **Dark Moon** <note>original: 晦 (hui); the final day of the lunar month when the moon is invisible.</note>. The "thread of survival and perishability" refers to that specific interval between the Dark Moon and the New Moon, when the **Yin** energy is about to be exhausted but is not yet gone, and the **Yang** energy is about to be born but has not yet emerged. However, these terms "Dark Moon" and "New Moon" are merely metaphors; the text does not literally mean the end of the thirtieth day or the start of the first day of a calendar month <note>Yu Yan is warning the reader not to wait for the actual moon in the sky, but to look for these "phases" within their own internal energetic state.</note>.

If perhaps the **Ruler** <note>A metaphor for the conscious Mind or the "Heart-Sovereign."</note> is arrogant and overflowing, reaching an extreme of fullness and violating the Way; or if perhaps the **Minister** <note>A metaphor for the Breath or the "Intention" that carries out the Mind's will.</note> is wicked and sycophantic, acting outside of the proper tracks—then the **Quarters and the Full Moon** <term>Quarters and Full Moon<note>original: 弦望 (xianwang); specifically the first quarter, the full moon, and the last quarter. These signify the balance points of the alchemical work.</note>, the waxing and waning, will fall into disorder...

<summary>Yu Yan explains that the movements of the sun and moon are metaphors for the internal "Fire Phasing" of alchemy, specifically highlighting the "Dark Moon" interval as a critical moment of transformation. He warns that if the practitioner's mind (Ruler) or intention (Minister) loses discipline, the internal energetic cycle will be disrupted.</summary>

# Transformation into Misfortune: The Enforcement of Law and the Burdening of the Master

The Sovereign is the **Spirit** <note>original: 神 (shen); the conscious and subconscious awareness that governs the body.</note>; the Minister is the **Breath** <note>original: 氣 (qi); the vital energy and physical respiration.</note>. When crafting the **Golden Elixir** <term>Golden Elixir<note>original: 金丹 (jindan); the ultimate goal of internal alchemy, representing a perfected state of being and longevity.</note>, **Lead and Mercury** <note>original: 鉛汞 (qiangong); symbolic terms for the fundamental energies of the body—often the primordial energy (Lead) and the volatile mind (Mercury).</note> must return to the **Earthen Cauldron** <note>original: 土釜 (tufu); a metaphor for the central point of the body where the transformation of energy occurs, usually the lower abdomen or "Dantian."</note>.

The body and mind must be silent and unmoving. If the body moves, the Breath scatters; if the mind moves, the Spirit scatters. One must congeal the Spirit and gather the Breath until the mind and the breath rely on each other. Only then can the **Spiritual Embryo** <note>original: 靈胎 (lingtai); the nascent "immortal self" or seed of enlightenment formed through meditation.</note> be formed. 

If this is not achieved, then the internal cycles of "Waxing and Waning" <note>original: 弦望 (xianwang); literally the quarter-moon and full-moon phases, referring to the rhythmic increase and decrease of energy within the practitioner.</note> will experience irregular expansion and contraction, perversely transforming into misfortune and blame. If we seek the cause of this failure, it is because the Sovereign Heart acts willfully and violates the **Dao** <note>The Way; the natural order of the universe.</note>. Consequently, the Breath also becomes treacherous and does not follow its proper orbit. This is why it is said: *The law is enforced, criticized, and interrogated for faults, bringing burden to the Master.* 

The **North Star** <note>original: 辰極 (chenji); representing the fixed, unmoving center of the self around which all other energies revolve.</note> must occupy its correct position, leisurefully presiding over all that is below.

The *Chapters on Returning to Destiny* <note>original: 復命篇 (Fuming Pian); a classic text of internal alchemy from the Southern School of Taoism.</note> states:

->The **Big Dipper** and the **Southern Stars** <note>original: 北斗南辰 (beidou nanchen); astronomical metaphors for the upper and lower poles of energy within the body.</note> descend to the edges of the eyebrows and eyelashes; with a focused heart, the movement of **Water and Fire** <note>The primary alchemical elements; Water represents the kidneys/essence and Fire represents the heart/spirit.</note> is stabilized.<-

<summary>Yu Yan explains that the Spirit must act as a stable ruler over the Breath to successfully form the "Spiritual Embryo." If the mind is restless, the internal energy cycles fall into chaos, leading to spiritual failure rather than refinement.</summary>
